-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload op add-surface

Add a board surface (docs/specs/surfacing.md, surface-levels.md): the whole stock taken down flat with a wide endmill, in straight passes back and forth, first in its setup, cut once. Flattens the spoilboard or faces the stock. A part’s area is op add surface with its shapes

chipload op add-surface <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--tool <TOOL>The endmill’s id in the project; default: the widest endmill.
--depth <DEPTH>How much comes off the top; default 0.5 mm.
--setup <SETUP>The setup whose board it faces (as setup list shows it); default: the active one.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload op rest-pass

A pocket’s rest passes (docs/specs/rest-passes.md): without –pass, list them; with it, change, turn off or on, or remove one

chipload op rest-pass <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--op <OP>The pocket’s number as the app lists it (1 = first) Required.
--pass <PASS>Which pass: 1 for the first (shown as 3.1 for operation 3)
--tool <TOOL>The tool’s id in the project: an endmill smaller than the tool before it.
--feed <FEED>
--plunge-feed <PLUNGE_FEED>
--step-down <STEP_DOWN>
--stepover <STEPOVER>A fraction of the tool’s diameter, at most 1.
--enabled <ENABLED>Cut it (true) or keep it without cutting (false) One of: true, false.
--removeRemove the pass; the passes after it rest after the tool before it.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload op make-rest-pass

Make a rest pocket that copies an earlier pocket (same shapes, depths, clearance and direction, rest after its tool) a rest pass of that pocket (docs/specs/rest-passes.md §5). It is then cut after the run of pockets it belongs to

chipload op make-rest-pass <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--op <OP>The rest pocket’s number as the app lists it (1 = first) Required.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload op part

Move an operation into another part, at the end of its operations (docs/specs/parts.md §7). Operations are numbered again in the new order

chipload op part <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--op <OP>The operation’s number as the app lists it (1 = first; get and set paths count from 0) Required.
--part <PART>The part’s id (as part list shows it) Required.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload op corners

List a profile’s or pocket’s corners on the tool’s side, with what each gets (round, dogbone, T-bone along the longer or shorter wall) and whether that is the corner’s own choice (docs/specs/dogbone-per-corner.md)

chipload op corners <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--op <OP>The operation’s number as the app lists it (1 = first) Required.

chipload op corner

Give one corner of a profile or pocket its own bone, whatever the operation’s corners say (docs/specs/dogbone-per-corner.md): --bone none keeps it round. op corners lists the corners

chipload op corner <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--op <OP>The operation’s number as the app lists it (1 = first) Required.
--at <AT>The corner, X,Y in drawing coordinates (a plain number is mm)
--bone <BONE>What the corner gets. One of: none, dogbone, tbone.
--along <ALONG>With a T-bone: the wall it runs along (the notch is in the other); default longer. One of: longer, shorter.
--as-operationRemove the corner’s own choice: it gets what the operation gives every corner.
--resetRemove every corner’s own choice.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload op tabs

List a profile’s tabs: each contour’s, with their centres on the tool path and whether they were placed by hand (docs/specs/tabs-by-hand.md). The job is planned. With –along or –around, first choose where contours without tabs placed by hand get theirs (docs/specs/tabs-along-grain.md)

chipload op tabs <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--op <OP>The operation’s number as the app lists it (1 = first) Required.
--along <ALONG>On the edges along this axis: x (bottom and top) or y (left and right), half on each. One of: x, y.
--aroundEvenly round each contour (as without –along)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload op tab

Place a profile’s tabs by hand (docs/specs/tabs-by-hand.md): add a tab at a point, or remove the one there. The first on a contour keeps that contour’s other tabs where Chipload put them. --even spaces one contour’s tabs evenly again, --reset all

chipload op tab <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--op <OP>The operation’s number as the app lists it (1 = first) Required.
--at <AT>The point, X,Y in drawing coordinates (a plain number is mm): on or near the contour, within a tool diameter.
--removeRemove the tab nearest to –at.
--evenPlace the tabs of the contour nearest to –at again, as the operation places them (evenly, or along an axis: op tabs --along)
--resetPlace every contour’s tabs again, as the operation places them.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload op rest

Make a pocket clear only what a larger tool leaves (docs/specs/rest-machining.md)

chipload op rest <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--op <OP>The pocket’s number as the app lists it (1 = first; get and set paths count from 0) Required.
--after-tool <AFTER_TOOL>The larger tool’s id in the project.
--noneClear the whole pocket again.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload shape

Draw in a project: add, change or delete circles, rectangles and polylines

chipload shape save

Save shapes and texts to the shape library under a name

chipload shape save <PROJECT> <IDS>... [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<IDS>The shape ids (as inspect lists them, without the S); a letter saves its whole text.
--texts <TEXTS>Texts to save, with their letters (ids as text list shows them) Repeatable.
--name <NAME>The entry’s name, with its group: “Signs/House number”. Required.
--replaceReplace an entry of that name (it is kept as .bak)

chipload shape insert

Insert an entry of the shape library into a project; prints the new ids

chipload shape insert <PROJECT> <NAME> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<NAME>The entry’s name, with its group: “Signs/House number”.
--at <AT>Where the middle of its box goes, X,Y in mm (default: the stock’s middle)
--around <AROUND>Scaled to fit round these shapes, –margin from their box (ids without the S) Repeatable.
--margin <MARGIN>With –around: from the shapes’ box to the entry’s, mm. Default: 10.
--width <WIDTH>Scaled to this width, mm; the height follows.
--height <HEIGHT>Scaled to this height, mm; the width follows.
--scale <SCALE>Scaled by this.
--layer <LAYER>Every shape and text on this layer (default: the layers they were saved on)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

The entries and groups (the default)

chipload shape library list

Where the library’s folder is

chipload shape library path

<TO>

<GROUP>

Groups of entries: folders in the library’s folder

<TO>

ArgumentMeaning
<NAME>

chipload shape similar

The shapes like one (docs/specs/fewer-clicks.md §3): for a circle, every circle of the same diameter on any layer; else the shapes of the same kind (closed or open) on the same layer whose boxes are the same size, either way round. Prints their ids, the shape itself included, for op add … --shapes

chipload shape similar <PROJECT> <ID>
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The shape id (as inspect lists it, without the S)

chipload shape inside

The closed shapes inside a closed shape, nested ones too, without it (docs/specs/fewer-clicks.md §3): a part’s pockets and holes. Prints their ids

chipload shape inside <PROJECT> <ID>
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The outline’s shape id (as inspect lists it, without the S)

chipload shape add

Add a drawn shape; prints its id

chipload shape add <PROJECT> <KIND> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<KIND>One of: circle, rectangle, polyline, polygon, spline (The smooth curve through –points (docs/specs/splines.md)), arc-slot (A slot bent round –center at –radius from –start-deg to –end-deg, –width wide: a closed polyline of arcs (docs/specs/drawing-improvements.md §3)).
--center <CENTER>Centre “X,Y” (circle, rectangle, polygon)
--radius <RADIUS>A circle’s radius; a polygon’s, to its corners.
--sides <SIDES>A polygon’s number of sides, 3 to 1000.
--across-flats <ACROSS_FLATS>A polygon’s size across its flats (twice the radius to the middle of a side), in place of –radius.
--length <LENGTH>Along the rectangle’s own X axis.
--width <WIDTH>Along the rectangle’s own Y axis.
--angle-deg <ANGLE_DEG>Rotation about the centre (a polygon: its first corner’s angle from +X), degrees, counter-clockwise.
--corner-radius <CORNER_RADIUS>The radius of the rectangle’s corners, at most half its shorter side (default 0: sharp)
--points <POINTS>Polyline points, or the points a spline passes through: “X,Y X,Y …”.
--bulges <BULGES>Polyline bulges “B B …”, one per segment: tan(sweep / 4) as in DXF, 0 straight, 1 a counter-clockwise half circle, -1 clockwise (default: all straight); t: an arc going on smoothly from the segment before it (docs/specs/polyline-tangent-arcs.md)
--start-deg <START_DEG>An arc slot’s start, degrees counter-clockwise from +X.
--end-deg <END_DEG>An arc slot’s end, degrees: past the start goes counter-clockwise, before it clockwise.
--radii <RADII>Corner radii “R R …”, mm, 0 sharp: a polyline’s one per point (an open one’s ends 0), a rectangle’s four counter-clockwise from its (−length/2, −width/2) corner, a polygon’s one per corner from its first. Each corner is rounded by an arc tangent to both sides, its point staying at the sharp corner (docs/specs/polyline-radii.md)
--openThe polyline or spline doesn’t return to its first point.
--closedThe polyline or spline returns to its first point (the default for a new polyline; a new spline is open without it)
--layer <LAYER>
--name <NAME>Its name in the Browser; “” takes the name away.
--construction <CONSTRUCTION>Construction geometry: shown and snapped to, never cut (--construction false makes it an ordinary shape again) One of: true, false.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload shape set

Change a drawn shape’s values or layer; it keeps its id

chipload shape set <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The shape id (as inspect lists it, without the S)
--center <CENTER>Centre “X,Y” (circle, rectangle, polygon)
--radius <RADIUS>A circle’s radius; a polygon’s, to its corners.
--sides <SIDES>A polygon’s number of sides, 3 to 1000.
--across-flats <ACROSS_FLATS>A polygon’s size across its flats (twice the radius to the middle of a side), in place of –radius.
--length <LENGTH>Along the rectangle’s own X axis.
--width <WIDTH>Along the rectangle’s own Y axis.
--angle-deg <ANGLE_DEG>Rotation about the centre (a polygon: its first corner’s angle from +X), degrees, counter-clockwise.
--corner-radius <CORNER_RADIUS>The radius of the rectangle’s corners, at most half its shorter side (default 0: sharp)
--points <POINTS>Polyline points, or the points a spline passes through: “X,Y X,Y …”.
--bulges <BULGES>Polyline bulges “B B …”, one per segment: tan(sweep / 4) as in DXF, 0 straight, 1 a counter-clockwise half circle, -1 clockwise (default: all straight); t: an arc going on smoothly from the segment before it (docs/specs/polyline-tangent-arcs.md)
--start-deg <START_DEG>An arc slot’s start, degrees counter-clockwise from +X.
--end-deg <END_DEG>An arc slot’s end, degrees: past the start goes counter-clockwise, before it clockwise.
--radii <RADII>Corner radii “R R …”, mm, 0 sharp: a polyline’s one per point (an open one’s ends 0), a rectangle’s four counter-clockwise from its (−length/2, −width/2) corner, a polygon’s one per corner from its first. Each corner is rounded by an arc tangent to both sides, its point staying at the sharp corner (docs/specs/polyline-radii.md)
--openThe polyline or spline doesn’t return to its first point.
--closedThe polyline or spline returns to its first point (the default for a new polyline; a new spline is open without it)
--layer <LAYER>
--name <NAME>Its name in the Browser; “” takes the name away.
--construction <CONSTRUCTION>Construction geometry: shown and snapped to, never cut (--construction false makes it an ordinary shape again) One of: true, false.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

ArgumentMeaning

<IDS>The shape ids (as inspect lists them, without the S)

chipload shape mirror

Mirror drawn shapes in place, about the middle of the box round them. They keep their ids, so the operations that cut them follow

chipload shape mirror <PROJECT> <IDS>... [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<IDS>The shape ids (as inspect lists them, without the S)
--axis <AXIS>x: flip the X coordinates (left becomes right); y: flip Y (up becomes down), about the middle of the box round them. One of: x, y.
--line <LINE>Mirror about the line through two points “X,Y X,Y” (docs/specs/drawing-improvements.md §1)
--about-shape <ABOUT_SHAPE>Mirror about drawn shape ID, one straight line (a construction centre line)
--copyKeep the shapes and add their mirror images; an open shape with both ends on the line joins its image into one closed shape.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload shape move

Move drawn shapes by –dx and –dy; they keep their ids, so the operations that cut them follow (docs/specs/drawn-shapes-box.md)

chipload shape move <PROJECT> <IDS>... [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<IDS>The shape ids (as inspect lists them, without the S)
--dx <DX>How far to move them right, mm. Required.
--dy <DY>How far to move them up, mm. Required.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload shape scale

Scale drawn shapes evenly about the middle of the box round them, or a point; or by reference: two points and the length their distance should have, the first point staying (docs/specs/scale-and-stretch.md §2)

chipload shape scale <PROJECT> <IDS>... [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<IDS>The shape ids (as inspect lists them, without the S)
--factor <FACTOR>The factor, greater than 0: 2 doubles them.
--about <ABOUT>Scale about this point “X,Y” instead.
--reference <REFERENCE>Two points “X1,Y1 X2,Y2”, on any shape: scaled so their distance becomes –length, the first point staying.
--length <LENGTH>With –reference: the length the two points’ distance becomes, mm (or with a unit)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload shape stretch

Stretch drawn shapes along X and Y about the middle of the box round them, or a point (docs/specs/scale-and-stretch.md §3). Circles and arcs become polylines fitted to the stretched curve; corner radii stay. Prints the shapes that became polylines

chipload shape stretch <PROJECT> <IDS>... [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<IDS>The shape ids (as inspect lists them, without the S)
--x <X>The factor along X, greater than 0 (default 1)
--y <Y>The factor along Y, greater than 0 (default 1)
--width <WIDTH>Stretch along X until the box round the shapes is this wide, mm (or with a unit)
--height <HEIGHT>Stretch along Y until the box round the shapes is this high, mm (or with a unit)
--about <ABOUT>The point that stays, “X,Y”; default: the middle of the box round the shapes.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload shape offset

Add the curve at a distance from drawn shapes, on their layers; the shapes stay (docs/specs/drawing-editing.md §2). Prints the new ids

chipload shape offset <PROJECT> <IDS>... [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<IDS>The shape ids (as inspect lists them, without the S)
--distance <DISTANCE>How far, mm (or with a unit) Required.
--side <SIDE>Which side: outside or inside of a closed shape, left or right of an open one (default: outside, or left) One of: outside, inside, left, right.
--corners <CORNERS>Round corners (the exact offset) or sharp ones (the sides extended to meet) One of: round, sharp. Default: round.
--count <COUNT>How many, each the distance further than the last. Default: 1.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload shape fillet

Round corners of a drawn shape with arcs of a radius, beside arcs too; it keeps its id (docs/specs/drawing-editing.md §3). With –with, a round between two open drawn polylines, lines or arcs instead, both trimmed or extended to it and joined as after a trim (docs/specs/fillet-between-shapes.md §5)

chipload shape fillet <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The shape id (as inspect lists it, without the S)
--radius <RADIUS>The radius, mm (or with a unit); 0 makes a corner sharp, or two shapes meet in one. Required.
--corner <CORNER>Only this corner: its point’s number, from 0 (default: every corner)
--with <WITH>The second shape: a round between the two.
--keep <KEEP>With –with, twice: a point near the part of each shape to keep, “X,Y”, in the shapes’ order. Repeatable.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload shape align

Line up drawn shapes: each one’s box side or middle with that of the first shape named, which stays put, or the stock’s; they move whole and keep their ids. The shapes of one contour, and a group named whole, line up as one (docs/specs/drawing-editing.md §7, arrange-whole.md, align-first.md)

--name <NAME>
--add <ADD>Shapes to add (any in another group move over) Repeatable.
--add-texts <ADD_TEXTS>Texts to add. Repeatable.
--remove <REMOVE>Shapes to take out; they stay where they are, free. Repeatable.
--remove-texts <REMOVE_TEXTS>Texts to take out. Repeatable.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload image

Pictures traced into shapes for V-carving: add, change, list, preview, remove

chipload image add

Add a picture (PNG, JPEG or BMP) and trace it into shapes on its own layer

chipload image add <PROJECT> <PICTURE> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<PICTURE>The picture.
--width <WIDTH>The picture’s width on the stock, mm; its height follows. Required.
--layer <LAYER>The layer for its shapes; default: the picture’s file name.
--invertCarve the light parts instead of the dark ones.
--at <AT>Where the picture’s lower-left corner goes: X,Y in mm (default 0,0)
--threshold <THRESHOLD>Grey values (0–255) below this are black: 1 to 254, or auto (default)
--blur <BLUR>Blur before the threshold, px, 0 to 10 (default 1)
--min-area <MIN_AREA>Drop specks smaller than this, mm², 0 to 100 (default 0.5)
--smoothing <SMOOTHING>How far an edge may be simplified, mm, 0.01 to 1 (default 0.05)
--trace <TRACE>What to trace: outline (for a V-carve, default) or centre-line (strokes, for a follow path)
--arcs <ARCS>Fit arcs as well as lines (true or false; default true) One of: true, false.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

Change an image’s settings, position or size, and trace it again

chipload image set <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The image’s id (as image list shows it)
--width <WIDTH>The picture’s width on the stock, mm.
--rotate <ROTATE>Turn it about its centre to this angle, degrees counter-clockwise (any angle; 0: as the picture is). --at stays the corner before the turn.
--layer <LAYER>The layer for its shapes.
--invert <INVERT>Carve the light parts instead (true or false) One of: true, false.
--at <AT>Where the picture’s lower-left corner goes: X,Y in mm (default 0,0)
--threshold <THRESHOLD>Grey values (0–255) below this are black: 1 to 254, or auto (default)
--blur <BLUR>Blur before the threshold, px, 0 to 10 (default 1)
--min-area <MIN_AREA>Drop specks smaller than this, mm², 0 to 100 (default 0.5)
--smoothing <SMOOTHING>How far an edge may be simplified, mm, 0.01 to 1 (default 0.05)
--trace <TRACE>What to trace: outline (for a V-carve, default) or centre-line (strokes, for a follow path)
--arcs <ARCS>Fit arcs as well as lines (true or false; default true) One of: true, false.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload model

3D models (STL) carved as reliefs from the top: add, change, list, remove (docs/specs/stl-relief.md)

chipload model add

Add an STL model, or a picture (PNG, JPEG, BMP) read as a depth map, white high, placed on the board; its footprint outline goes on its own layer

chipload model add <PROJECT> <MODEL> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<MODEL>The STL file (binary or text), or a picture.
--units <UNITS>The unit the file is in, for its own size: mm (default) or inch. The model is placed by --width anyway.
--layer <LAYER>The layer for its outline; default: the file’s name.
--at <AT>Where the footprint’s lower-left corner goes: X,Y in mm (default 0,0)
--width <WIDTH>The footprint’s width on the board, mm (default: the model’s own); X and Y scale alike.
--depth <DEPTH>How deep the relief goes, mm (default: in proportion to the width; a picture 4 mm)
--rotate <ROTATE>Turn it about its footprint’s centre to this angle, degrees counter-clockwise.
--up <UP>Which of the model’s axes points up: z (default), y, x, -z, -y or -x.
--keep <KEEP>How much of the model to keep, from its top: a share (0.5) or a percentage (50%); the rest, its back, is cut away (default: all of it). Width and depth are the kept part’s.
--inflateInflate the picture’s black-and-white shapes, each rising from its edge, instead of reading it as a depth map (docs/specs/inflated-shapes.md)
--mode <MODE>How the picture becomes heights: depth (a depth map) or inflate (its shapes)
--threshold <THRESHOLD>Inflated: grey levels below this (1 to 254) are black; auto: picked from the picture (default)
--invert <INVERT>Inflated: white is the shape (true) or black is (false, the default) One of: true, false.
--profile <PROFILE>Inflated: how a shape rises from its edge: round (default) or straight.
--reach <REACH>Inflated: how far in from the edge the height grows, in mm; auto: the widest shape’s half-width (default)
--white-high <WHITE_HIGH>White is high (true, the default) or black is (false) One of: true, false.
--smoothing <SMOOTHING>A blur over the heights, its radius on the board: 0 to 5 mm (default 0.2)
--background <BACKGROUND>Grey shares up to this are background, not the model: 0 to 0.95 or 95% (default 0)
--gamma <GAMMA>The heights raised to this power, 0.2 to 5: above 1 flatter low ground and steeper tops (default 1)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload model set

Change a model’s place, size, depth, up axis or cut level, or a picture’s depth-map settings; its outline is drawn again

chipload model set <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The model’s id (as model list shows it)
--layer <LAYER>The layer for its outline.
--proportionalMake the depth follow the width again, in proportion.
--at <AT>Where the footprint’s lower-left corner goes: X,Y in mm (default 0,0)
--width <WIDTH>The footprint’s width on the board, mm (default: the model’s own); X and Y scale alike.
--depth <DEPTH>How deep the relief goes, mm (default: in proportion to the width; a picture 4 mm)
--rotate <ROTATE>Turn it about its footprint’s centre to this angle, degrees counter-clockwise.
--up <UP>Which of the model’s axes points up: z (default), y, x, -z, -y or -x.
--keep <KEEP>How much of the model to keep, from its top: a share (0.5) or a percentage (50%); the rest, its back, is cut away (default: all of it). Width and depth are the kept part’s.
--inflateInflate the picture’s black-and-white shapes, each rising from its edge, instead of reading it as a depth map (docs/specs/inflated-shapes.md)
--mode <MODE>How the picture becomes heights: depth (a depth map) or inflate (its shapes)
--threshold <THRESHOLD>Inflated: grey levels below this (1 to 254) are black; auto: picked from the picture (default)
--invert <INVERT>Inflated: white is the shape (true) or black is (false, the default) One of: true, false.
--profile <PROFILE>Inflated: how a shape rises from its edge: round (default) or straight.
--reach <REACH>Inflated: how far in from the edge the height grows, in mm; auto: the widest shape’s half-width (default)
--white-high <WHITE_HIGH>White is high (true, the default) or black is (false) One of: true, false.
--smoothing <SMOOTHING>A blur over the heights, its radius on the board: 0 to 5 mm (default 0.2)
--background <BACKGROUND>Grey shares up to this are background, not the model: 0 to 0.95 or 95% (default 0)
--gamma <GAMMA>The heights raised to this power, 0.2 to 5: above 1 flatter low ground and steeper tops (default 1)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload text

Text written into shapes for V-carving, pocketing or profiling: add, change, list, remove, and the built-in fonts (docs/specs/text.md)

chipload text add

Write a text into shapes on its own layer; prints its id. “\n” in the text starts a new line

chipload text add <PROJECT> <TEXT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<TEXT>What it says.
--height <HEIGHT>The height of a capital letter, mm. Required.
--at <AT>Where the text’s anchor goes: X,Y in mm (default 0,0)
--anchor <ANCHOR>What –at is: the lower-left corner of the text’s box (default) or its centre. A centred text stays centred when it changes. Changed without –at, the text stays where it is. One of: corner, center.
--rotate <ROTATE>Turn it about its centre to this angle, degrees counter-clockwise (any angle). --at stays the corner before the turn.
--font <FONT>A built-in font (text fonts; default FreeSans)
--align <ALIGN>How the lines line up (default left) One of: left, center, right.
--spacing <SPACING>Extra space between letters, mm (default 0: the font’s own)
--line-spacing <LINE_SPACING>From one line’s base to the next, in capital heights (default 1.6)
--layer <LAYER>The layer for its letters (default “text”)
--arc <ARC>Write it along a circle round –at of this radius, through the letters’ middles (docs/specs/text-on-arc.md); a new text on an arc is centred on its angle unless –align says otherwise.
--side <SIDE>On an arc: along the top (read clockwise, the default) or the bottom (upright, read counter-clockwise) One of: top, bottom.
--angle <ANGLE>On an arc: where it sits, degrees counter-clockwise from +X (its middle, start or end as aligned; default 90 on the top, 270 on the bottom)
--straightOff the arc: on straight lines again.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload text set

Change a text (what it says, its font, size, place or turn) and write it again

ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The text’s id (as text list shows it)
--text <TEXT>What it says.
--height <HEIGHT>The height of a capital letter, mm.
--at <AT>Where the text’s anchor goes: X,Y in mm (default 0,0)
--anchor <ANCHOR>What –at is: the lower-left corner of the text’s box (default) or its centre. A centred text stays centred when it changes. Changed without –at, the text stays where it is. One of: corner, center.
--rotate <ROTATE>Turn it about its centre to this angle, degrees counter-clockwise (any angle). --at stays the corner before the turn.
--font <FONT>A built-in font (text fonts; default FreeSans)
--align <ALIGN>How the lines line up (default left) One of: left, center, right.
--spacing <SPACING>Extra space between letters, mm (default 0: the font’s own)
--line-spacing <LINE_SPACING>From one line’s base to the next, in capital heights (default 1.6)
--layer <LAYER>The layer for its letters (default “text”)
--arc <ARC>Write it along a circle round –at of this radius, through the letters’ middles (docs/specs/text-on-arc.md); a new text on an arc is centred on its angle unless –align says otherwise.
--side <SIDE>On an arc: along the top (read clockwise, the default) or the bottom (upright, read counter-clockwise) One of: top, bottom.
--angle <ANGLE>On an arc: where it sits, degrees counter-clockwise from +X (its middle, start or end as aligned; default 90 on the top, 270 on the bottom)
--straightOff the arc: on straight lines again.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload frame

Frames for signs, drawn round shapes or to a size, with border lines (docs/specs/shape-library.md)

chipload frame kinds

The built-in frames, and what their corner size means

chipload frame kinds

chipload frame add

Draw a frame round shapes (–around, –around-text) or to a size (–width and –height), with border lines inside it; prints the new shapes’ ids

chipload frame add <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--kind <KIND>Which frame (frame kinds); default rectangle, with –corners. One of: rectangle, rounded, notched, chamfered, stepped, oval, arched, double-arch, gable, shield, plaque, banner, octagon.
--around <AROUND>Round these shapes (ids as inspect lists them, without the S): the frame is centred on their box, –margin from it to the innermost line. Repeatable.
--around-text <AROUND_TEXT>Round these texts’ letters (ids as text list shows them) Repeatable.
--around-image <AROUND_IMAGE>Round these traced pictures’ shapes (ids as image list shows them); the frame follows a picture traced again. Repeatable.
--margin <MARGIN>From the shapes’ box to the innermost line, mm (default 10)
--width <WIDTH>The outline’s width, mm, when not round shapes.
--height <HEIGHT>The outline’s height, mm, when not round shapes.
--at <AT>Its centre, X,Y in mm (default: the stock’s centre), with –width and –height.
--corner <CORNER>The corner size, mm (default: a tenth of the shorter side; frame kinds says what it is for each frame)
--borders <BORDERS>Border lines inside the outline, 0 to 3. Default: 0.
--gap <GAP>From the outline to the first border and between borders, mm. Default: 5.
--corners <CORNERS>A rectangular frame’s corners: sharp, round, notch, chamfer or step (with –kind rectangle, or instead of it) One of: sharp, round, notch, chamfer, step.
--border-corners <BORDER_CORNERS>The borders’ own corners, inside a rectangular frame (default: the outline’s shape) One of: sharp, round, notch, chamfer, step.
--doubleEach border two lines, twice –border-width apart.
--border-width <BORDER_WIDTH>A border’s groove width at the surface, mm: the margin is measured to its inner edge (default 0: lines) Default: 0.
--layer <LAYER>The outline’s layer. Default: frame.
--border-layer <BORDER_LAYER>The borders’ layer. Default: border.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

<ID>Its outline’s or a border’s shape id.
--kind <KIND>Another frame kind (frame kinds) One of: rectangle, rounded, notched, chamfered, stepped, oval, arched, double-arch, gable, shield, plaque, banner, octagon.
--corner <CORNER>The corner size, mm.
--margin <MARGIN>From what it goes round to the innermost line, mm.
--borders <BORDERS>Border lines inside the outline, 0 to 3.
--gap <GAP>From the outline to the first border and between borders, mm.
--corners <CORNERS>A rectangular frame’s corners: sharp, round, notch, chamfer or step. One of: sharp, round, notch, chamfer, step.
--border-corners <BORDER_CORNERS>The borders’ own corners, inside a rectangular frame. One of: sharp, round, notch, chamfer, step.
--border-corners-offThe borders follow the outline’s shape again.
--double <DOUBLE>Each border two lines (true) or one (false) One of: true, false.
--border-width <BORDER_WIDTH>A border’s groove width at the surface, mm.
--width <WIDTH>A frame drawn to a size: its outline’s width, mm.
--height <HEIGHT>A frame drawn to a size: its outline’s height, mm.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload sign

A sign in one step: words, a frame that follows them, and every operation that cuts it (docs/specs/sign-maker.md)

chipload sign add

Make a sign: the words centred on the board, a frame round them that follows them, the sign grouped in a part of its own, and its operations: the letters, the borders’ grooves, the edge chamfer, and the profile that cuts it out

chipload sign add <PROJECT> <WORDS> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<WORDS>What it says; “\n” starts a new line. Leave it out to make the sign round shapes already drawn (–around, –around-text, –around-image)
--list <LIST>A list of signs instead of the words: a text file, one sign per line, | (or “\n”) starting a sign’s second line. Every sign gets the same frame and style, in one part whose operations cut them all (docs/specs/batch-signs.md)
--list-words <LIST_WORDS>The list given here instead of a file: signs separated by “\n”, lines by |.
--list-gap <LIST_GAP>With a list: between two signs, and from a kept board’s edge, mm (default 10)
--around <AROUND>Round these shapes instead of new words (ids as inspect lists them) Repeatable.
--around-text <AROUND_TEXT>Round these texts’ letters. Repeatable.
--around-image <AROUND_IMAGE>Round these traced pictures (ids as image list shows them): carved as the picture, the frame following it when it is traced again. Repeatable.
--font <FONT>The font (chipload text fonts)
--height <HEIGHT>The capital height, mm. Default: 30.
--size <SIZE>The sign’s outer size “W×H”, such as 300x100 (any length unit; plain numbers are mm): the frame is that size and the letters as high as fit, instead of –height (docs/specs/sign-size-and-start.md §1)
--engrave-depth <ENGRAVE_DEPTH>With a single-line font (text fonts): how deep the follow path engraves the words, mm (default 1)
--frame <FRAME>The frame (frame kinds) One of: rectangle, rounded, notched, chamfered, stepped, oval, arched, double-arch, gable, shield, plaque, banner, octagon. Default: rounded.
--corners <CORNERS>A rectangular frame’s corners: sharp, round, notch, chamfer or step. One of: sharp, round, notch, chamfer, step.
--corner <CORNER>The frame’s corner size, mm (default: a tenth of its shorter side)
--margin <MARGIN>From the words to the inner edge of the innermost border’s groove, mm. Default: 10.
--borders <BORDERS>Border lines inside the outline, 0 to 3. Default: 1.
--gap <GAP>From the outline to the first border and between borders, mm. Default: 5.
--border-corners <BORDER_CORNERS>The borders’ own corners, inside a rectangular frame. One of: sharp, round, notch, chamfer, step.
--doubleEach border two grooves.
--roundRound words, with --frame oval: the first line along the top of a circle, the second along its bottom, the rest straight in the middle; the frame the circle round them (docs/specs/text-on-arc.md §6)
--border-width <BORDER_WIDTH>The borders’ groove width at the surface, mm: their depth comes from the V-bit.
--style <STYLE>engraved (V-carved letters), raised (the background cleared round them) or inlay. One of: engraved, raised, inlay. Default: engraved.
--relief <RELIEF>A raised sign’s background depth, mm.
--edge <EDGE>The outline’s edge: none, or chamfer:WIDTH (mm) cut with the V-bit. Default: none.
--vbit <VBIT>The V-bit for the letters and grooves (a project tool id) Required.
--end-mill <END_MILL>The end mill that cuts the sign out (and clears a raised background) Required.
--thickness <THICKNESS>The board’s thickness, mm; the board is fitted round the sign.
--board-margin <BOARD_MARGIN>From the sign’s outline to the board’s edges, mm.
--keep-stockLeave the stock as it is instead of fitting it round the sign.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload relief

A relief in one step: a 3D model turned, cut and sized, its relief, a roughing pass, and what is round it (docs/specs/relief-wizard.md)

chipload relief add

Make a relief: the model (an STL file, or –model for one in the project) turned, cut and sized, centred on the board, its relief with an optional roughing pass, and what is round it, in a part of its own and in cutting order

chipload relief add <PROJECT> <FILE> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<FILE>The STL file; leave it out with –model.
--model <MODEL>A model already in the project (an id from model list), in place of a file.
--units <UNITS>The unit a new file is read in: mm (default) or inch.
--up <UP>Which of the model’s axes points up: z (default), y, x, -z, -y or -x.
--rotate <ROTATE>Turned on the board, degrees counter-clockwise.
--keep <KEEP>How much of it to keep from the top: 0.5 or 50% (default: all)
--width <WIDTH>Its width on the board, mm (default: half the board’s)
--depth <DEPTH>How deep the relief goes, mm (default: in proportion to the width)
--at <AT>Where its footprint’s lower-left corner goes, X,Y (default: centred on the board)
--start-depth <START_DEPTH>How far below the board’s surface the model’s top lies (default 0): for an unplaned board. The rim’s ramp and the roughing start at the surface.
--around <AROUND>What goes round it. One of: rim (A flat round the model’s outline at the relief’s floor, then a ramp up to the stock top), box (The same round the rectangle round the model), cut-out (No rim: a profile outside the outline, through the board, with tabs). Default: rim.
--margin <MARGIN>The rim’s flat round the outline or the model’s box, mm (default 5, or wider: the roughing tool’s diameter and its allowance on both sides)
--ramp <RAMP>The ramp from the flat up to the stock top, degrees from horizontal: 10 to 90 (default 45; 90: a straight wall)
--hull <HULL>How far an outline rim is filled out towards the model’s convex hull: 0 (default, the outline as it is) to 1 or 100% (the hull)
--corner <CORNER>A box rim’s corner radius, mm, at most the margin (default 0: sharp)
--tool <TOOL>The ball nose that finishes it (a project tool id) Required.
--rough-tool <ROUGH_TOOL>An end mill for a roughing pass first (a project tool id)
--allowance <ALLOWANCE>What the roughing pass leaves for the finish, mm.
--smoothA smoothing pass after the roughing, with the roughing tool, its lines across the roughing’s: it takes the steps the roughing leaves on slopes.
--end-mill <END_MILL>The end mill for the cut-out (default: the roughing one)
--inflateInflate the picture’s black-and-white shapes, each rising from its edge, instead of reading it as a depth map (docs/specs/inflated-shapes.md)
--mode <MODE>How the picture becomes heights: depth (a depth map) or inflate (its shapes)
--threshold <THRESHOLD>Inflated: grey levels below this (1 to 254) are black; auto: picked from the picture (default)
--invert <INVERT>Inflated: white is the shape (true) or black is (false, the default) One of: true, false.
--profile <PROFILE>Inflated: how a shape rises from its edge: round (default) or straight.
--reach <REACH>Inflated: how far in from the edge the height grows, in mm; auto: the widest shape’s half-width (default)
--white-high <WHITE_HIGH>White is high (true, the default) or black is (false) One of: true, false.
--smoothing <SMOOTHING>A blur over the heights, its radius on the board: 0 to 5 mm (default 0.2)
--background <BACKGROUND>Grey shares up to this are background, not the model: 0 to 0.95 or 95% (default 0)
--gamma <GAMMA>The heights raised to this power, 0.2 to 5: above 1 flatter low ground and steeper tops (default 1)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Write here instead of over the project.

chipload dxf

A project’s DXFs: add another, list them, remove one (docs/specs/drawing-sources.md)

chipload dxf add

Add a DXF or an SVG to the project, where the file has it; move it with place --dxf ID (an SVG shares the DXFs’ numbers and commands)

chipload dxf add <PROJECT> <DXF> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<DXF>The DXF or SVG (.svg, .svgz)
--units <UNITS>The DXF’s units, if it doesn’t say.
--px-per-inch <PX_PER_INCH>An SVG’s pixels per inch, if it gives no real size: 96 (Inkscape, browsers) or 72 (older Illustrator)
--name <NAME>Its name; default: the file’s name without its extension.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload dxf list

The project’s DXFs: id, name, file, placement and shapes

chipload dxf list <PROJECT>
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)

chipload dxf explode

Turn a DXF’s shapes into drawn shapes you can edit; the project no longer knows the DXF (docs/specs/explode-and-join.md). Operations keep cutting the same shapes

chipload dxf explode <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The DXF’s id (as dxf list shows it); not needed when the project has one.
--join <JOIN>Then join ends closer than this (as shape join --gap), e.g. 0.01 or 0.05mm.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload dxf export

Write the drawing as a DXF: every shape the canvas shows, where it shows it, on its layers, in mm (docs/specs/dxf-export.md). The file is read back before it is written

chipload dxf export <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>The DXF to write. Required.
--inchIn inches instead of mm.
--hiddenHidden groups and layers too.
--layer <LAYERS>Only this layer (repeat for more) Repeatable.
--shape <SHAPES>Only these shapes, by id (as inspect lists them, without the S) Repeatable.
--constructionConstruction geometry too, each on a layer of its own (LAYER_construction)
--piecesThe active setup’s pieces instead of the drawing, each on a layer named after it (docs/specs/pieces.md §7)

chipload dxf remove

Remove a DXF and its shapes

chipload dxf remove <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The DXF’s id (as dxf list shows it)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

ArgumentMeaning

--rpm <RPM>

chipload style set

Change a style: values of its operation by path, as chipload set changes a project’s (end_depth=6, tabs.count=6, side=on; style show lists them), and its tool by name from your library, for example after an import whose tool you don’t have. Projects that used the style keep their operations

chipload style set <NAME> <VALUES>... [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<NAME>
<VALUES>PATH=VALUE, paths within the style’s operation.
--tool <TOOL>Another tool of your library, by name.
--layer <LAYER>A layer rule: the style is used on every layer whose name matches, * any run of characters, ? one, upper or lower case alike (CUT*, DRILL_?); “” for none (docs/specs/fewer-clicks.md §4)

chipload style apply

Apply the layer rules to a project: each style with a layer pattern adds its operations for the layers it matches, in the Styles’ order, then by layer name. A layer an operation already cuts is skipped, so applying twice adds nothing. import does this by itself unless –no-rules

ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--dry-runSay what would be added, write nothing.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload part set

Rename a part, or turn it off or on (a part that is off isn’t cut)

chipload part set <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The part’s id (as part list shows it)
--name <NAME>Its new name.
--offLeave the part and its operations out of the program.
--onCut the part again.
--mirror <MIRROR>Cut the part mirrored about the centre line of its box, the drawing as drawn: left-right, top-bottom, or none (docs/specs/mirror-part.md). Climb stays climb.
--order <ORDER>The order its instances are cut in (docs/specs/instances.md §2) One of: by-operation (Each operation on every instance, then the next operation), by-instance (Every operation on one instance, then the next (was by-copy, by-part)).
--turns <TURNS>How it may be turned when nested: none, half, quarter or every-15 (docs/specs/nesting.md §3)
--material <MATERIAL>The library material it is cut from (docs/specs/parts-and-setups.md §4); a board of another material gets a note (–move moves it)
--thickness <THICKNESS>The thickness it is cut from (any length unit; plain numbers are mm); a board of another thickness gets a note (–move moves it)
--stock <STOCK>“none”: cut from any board again. One of: none.
--moveMove it, every instance, to a setup of its stock: the first that has it, else a new one made like its board.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload part place

Place instance 1, the part as first placed: cut moved and turned, its shapes staying where they are drawn (docs/specs/part-placement.md); the same as part instance set … 1

chipload part place <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--part <PART>Which part (as part list shows it); needed when the project has several.
--at <AT>Its move “DX,DY” from where it is drawn (any length unit; plain numbers are mm)
--angle <ANGLE>Turned about the middle of the part’s box, degrees counter-clockwise.
--resetBack where drawn: not moved, not turned (then –at and –angle, if given)
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

--at <AT>Its move “DX,DY” from the part as drawn (any length unit; plain numbers are mm); default: beside everything the part cuts, 10 mm away.
--angle <ANGLE>Turned about the middle of the part’s box, degrees counter-clockwise.
--mirror <MIRROR>Mirrored about the middle of the part’s box first: left-right, top-bottom or none. Climb stays climb.
--like <LIKE>Like this instance (its number): on its setup, turned and mirrored as it is; –setup, –angle and –mirror still override each.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload part instance array

The part’s instances made a grid: its cells but the first, in place of the instances it has, numbered row by row from 2 (what chipload copies does)

chipload part instance array <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--part <PART>Which part; needed when the project has several.
--setup <SETUP>The setup (board) it goes on, as setup list shows it; default: the one the part is cut from.
--grid <GRID>Columns x rows, e.g. “3x2”. Required.
--pitch <PITCH>Pitch “DX,DY” from cell to cell; negative grows left or down.
--gap <GAP>Set the pitch so that this many mm are left between the cuts of neighbours.
--order <ORDER>The cut order. One of: by-operation (Each operation on every instance, then the next operation), by-instance (Every operation on one instance, then the next (was by-copy, by-part)).
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload setup

A project’s setups: each a board clamped once, with its stock, work zero and parts, run as a program of its own (docs/specs/setups.md)

chipload setup list

The project’s setups: id, name, on or off, board, work zero and parts

chipload setup list <PROJECT>
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)

chipload setup add

Add a setup at the end, an empty board like the active one’s, which becomes active; parts are placed on it afterwards. A back (–back-of) gets a first part of its own

chipload setup add <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--name <NAME>Its name; default: “Setup N”.
--thickness <THICKNESS>Its board’s thickness; default: the active setup’s.
--back-of <BACK_OF>The back of this setup: the same board turned over, its cuts mirrored (docs/specs/two-sided.md). Its name defaults to that setup’s name and “back” (“Setup 1 back”)
--turn <TURN>How the board is turned over: left-right (default) or top-bottom.
--register <REGISTER>How the turned board is put back: pins (default) or corner.
--pins <PINS>Dowel holes for pins of this diameter too (8 mm without a value), in the same step (docs/specs/two-sided-wizard.md §6)
--pin-depth <PIN_DEPTH>How far the dowel holes go into the spoilboard; default 8 mm.
--zeroSet the front’s work zero to the first pin’s centre, which the back shares.
--pin-tool <PIN_TOOL>The project tool that cuts the dowel holes; default: a drill of their size, else the largest end mill that bores them.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload setup set

Rename a setup, or turn it off or on (a setup that is off isn’t cut)

chipload setup set <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The setup’s id (as setup list shows it)
--name <NAME>Its new name.
--offLeave the setup and its parts out.
--onCut the setup again.
--back-of <BACK_OF>Make it the back of this setup: the same board turned over.
--own-boardGive a back a board of its own again, as it is now.
--turn <TURN>How a back is turned over: left-right or top-bottom.
--register <REGISTER>How a turned board is located: pins (dowel pins on the turn axis) or corner.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload setup dowels

Dowel holes for a back setup (docs/specs/two-sided.md §6): two circles on the line the board turns about, in the margin beyond the shapes, cut first with the front, through the board and into the spoilboard for the pins

chipload setup dowels <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--setup <SETUP>The back setup’s id. Required.
--diameter <DIAMETER>The pins’ diameter; default 8 mm (a 6 mm end mill bores it)
--depth <DEPTH>How far the holes go into the spoilboard; default 8 mm.
--zeroSet the front’s work zero to the first pin’s centre, which the back shares.
--tool <TOOL>The project tool that cuts them; default: a drill of their size, else the largest end mill that bores them.
--removeRemove the dowel holes instead: the front’s operation and its circles.
--placePlace the dowel holes there are again, clear of both sides’ cuts as they are now; a work zero at the first pin moves with it. Not once the front is cut: its holes are drilled.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload setup pieces

Cut a setup as a puzzle: pieces that each fit a board, joined at seams with puzzle knobs or fingers, each piece a program of its own (docs/specs/pieces.md). Values not given stay as they are, or take the joint’s defaults the first time

chipload setup pieces <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--setup <SETUP>The setup’s id; default: the active setup.
--board <BOARD>The boards’ size, X×Y, such as 600x400; default: the machine’s travel.
--grid <GRID>Columns × rows of pieces, such as 3x2, or auto: the fewest that fit the board.
--joint <JOINT>The joint along the seams: knobs (default) or fingers.
--size <SIZE>The joint’s size: a knob’s head diameter (default 30 mm), a finger’s length (15 mm)
--spacing <SPACING>How far apart joints are along a seam (default 120 mm for knobs, 60 mm for fingers)
--clearance <CLEARANCE>The room between pieces at a seam, for glue; default 0.2 mm.
--shift-x <SHIFT_X>Move a seam between columns along X: SEAM=LENGTH, seams counted from the left from 1, such as 2=15. Repeat for more. Repeatable.
--shift-y <SHIFT_Y>Move a seam between rows along Y: SEAM=LENGTH, seams counted from the bottom. Repeatable.
--offCut the setup as one board again.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload setup activate

Make a setup the active one: shown, simulated, where new parts go

chipload setup activate <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The setup’s id.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload setup part

Move a part into a setup: it is cut from that board, with its zero

chipload setup part <PROJECT> <PART> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<PART>The part’s id (as part list shows it)
--to <TO>The setup’s id. Required.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload setup remove

Remove a setup. Refused while it has parts, unless –with-parts

chipload setup remove <PROJECT> <ID> [OPTIONS]

<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
<ID>The setup’s id.
--with-partsRemove its parts and their operations with it.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload zero

Where the G-code’s X0 Y0 Z0 is (docs/specs/work-zero-marker.md §5): a corner or the centre of the stock, the drawing origin, or a point. Moving it moves no cut, only the program’s numbers. Without an option, print where it is

chipload zero <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--stock <STOCK>At a corner or the centre of the stock; it follows the stock’s size. One of: bottom-left, bottom-right, top-left, top-right, center.
--drawing-originAt the drawing’s origin.
--at <AT>At the point “X,Y” in drawing coordinates (any length unit; plain numbers are mm)
--z <Z>Z0 at the stock’s top or bottom. One of: top, bottom.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload copies

Cut a part several times in a grid: its instances made a grid, the same as part instance array; –off is part instance remove --all (docs/specs/instances.md §5)

chipload copies <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--part <PART>Which part (as part list shows it); needed when the project has several.
--grid <GRID>Columns x rows, e.g. “3x2”.
--pitch <PITCH>Pitch “DX,DY” in mm, from cell to cell; negative grows left or down.
--gap <GAP>Set the pitch so that this many mm are left between the cuts of neighbours.
--order <ORDER>The cut order. One of: by-operation (Each operation on every instance, then the next operation), by-instance (Every operation on one instance, then the next (was by-copy, by-part)).
--offNo instances: the part is cut once.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload nest

Nest a setup: every instance of its parts placed on its board as close as they fit, apart by the gap, what doesn’t fit switched off (docs/specs/nesting.md)

chipload nest <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--setup <SETUP>The setup (as setup list shows it); default: the active one.
--board <BOARD>The board’s size “X×Y”, such as 1220x610 (any length unit; plain numbers are mm). Needed when the board follows the drawing: it becomes this size, its corner at the drawing’s origin.
--gap <GAP>The least distance between what two instances cut (default: as last nested, else 3 mm)
--margin <MARGIN>The room left at the board’s edge (default: as last nested, else 10 mm)
--turns <TURNS>How parts may be turned: none, half, quarter or every-15, for every part; “ID=TURNS” for one part, such as 2=none (wood grain). Kept in the project. Default: as set, else quarter. Repeatable.
--count <COUNT>How many times part ID is cut, “ID=N”, such as 2=4: instances added or removed before nesting (never the part itself) Repeatable.
--overflow <OVERFLOW>What doesn’t fit always goes onto more boards: the next setup with the same stock, else a new one like this one (docs/specs/nest-overflow-and-all.md §1). Kept for scripts: new-boards is what happens anyway; off is refused. One of: off (No longer offered: refused with the reason), new-boards (What happens anyway).
--goal <GOAL>What the nest makes as small as it can: compact (the smallest box round the parts), low (the lowest top edge: a strip across the top left free) or left (the leftmost right edge: a strip down the right left free). Kept in the project. Default: as last nested, else compact (docs/specs/nesting-search.md) One of: compact (The smallest box round the parts), low (The lowest top edge), left (The leftmost right edge).
--allNest every setup again as one pool per stock, the first board filled first, each with its kept settings; what fits on none onto new boards (docs/specs/nest-overflow-and-all.md §2)
--dry-runReport what would be placed without writing the project.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload place

Move, turn or scale the imported drawing (docs/specs/drawing-placement.md, drawing-rotation.md, drawing-scale.md). Without an option, print where it is

chipload place <PROJECT> [OPTIONS]
ArgumentMeaning
<PROJECT>The project (.chipload)
--by <BY>Move by “DX,DY” mm.
--to <TO>Put it “DX,DY” mm from where the DXF has it; 0,0 is as drawn.
--at <AT>Put the lower-left corner of its bounding box at “X,Y” mm (the app’s Position)
--align <ALIGN>Align the drawing’s extent with the stock. One of: bottom-left (Bottom-left corner on the stock’s, –margin mm in), center (Centre on the stock’s centre).
--margin <MARGIN>With –align bottom-left: how far in from the stock’s corner, mm.
--rotate <ROTATE>Turn it about its centre by this many degrees counter-clockwise (negative: clockwise), any angle, on top of any turn it has. Before –align when both are given.
--scale <SCALE>Scale it about its centre by this factor (> 0), on top of any scale it has. After –rotate, before –align.
--width <WIDTH>Scale it about its centre until it is this wide, mm (the height follows)
--height <HEIGHT>Scale it about its centre until it is this high, mm (the width follows)
--reference <REFERENCE>Two points “X1,Y1 X2,Y2”: scale it so their distance becomes –length, the first point staying (docs/specs/scale-and-stretch.md §2). After –rotate, before –align.
--length <LENGTH>With –reference: the length the two points’ distance becomes, mm (or with a unit)
--resetBack to where the DXF has it: no turn, no scale, no move.
--dxf <DXF>Which DXF of the project (as dxf list shows); needed when it has several.
--image <IMAGE>A picture of the project instead (as image list shows it): –at, –rotate and –width apply, as image set.
--text <TEXT>A text of the project instead (as text list shows it): –at (its anchor: the corner or the centre, as set), –rotate and –height (its capital height) apply, as text set.
-o, --output <OUTPUT>Where to write the result; default: the project itself.

chipload schema

Print the JSON Schema of a file format (docs/specs/scripting.md §2)

chipload schema <FORMAT>
ArgumentMeaning
