chipload mcp

Serve the commands as tools to an AI assistant over the Model Context Protocol, on stdin and stdout (e.g. claude mcp add chipload -- chipload mcp)

chipload mcp

Project file

A project (.chipload) is a JSON file. Chipload writes every field; when you write one by hand or with a script, the fields marked no may be left out. Lengths are in mm, angles in radians, feeds in mm/min. chipload schema project prints the JSON Schema this page is made from.

Schema version 43. Older files load through Chipload’s migrations; this schema describes only the current version.

The file

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
active_partinteger or nullnoThe part new operations go into (its id). Null or no such part: the first part. Only the app and the command line read it; it changes nothing that is cut.
active_setupinteger or nullnoThe setup shown, simulated and previewed, where new parts go (its id). Null or no such setup: the first. Only the app and the command line read it.
drawingDrawingyesThe drawing: the shapes Chipload cuts, imported from a DXF or drawn in Chipload.
machineMachineyesThe machine: post-processor, safe height, limits.
operationslist of OperationyesThe operations, cut in this order: grouped by part, in the parts’ order.
partslist of PartyesThe parts: groups of operations, each cut with its copies before the next part. At least one.
schema_version43yesThe version of the file format. Chipload upgrades older files when it reads them, and refuses newer ones.
setupslist of SetupyesThe setups: each a board clamped on the machine, with its stock, its work zero and the parts cut from it, written as a program of its own. At least one. Since schema 34.
toolslist of ToolyesThe tools the operations cut with.
written_bystring or nullnoThe Chipload version that wrote the file, for reference.

ArcSegment

From start_angle through sweep radians (positive = counter-clockwise).

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
center2 × numberyesCentre, mm.
radiusnumberyesRadius, mm.
start_anglenumberyesWhere the arc starts, radians from +X, counter-clockwise.
sweepnumberyesHow far it turns, radians: positive counter-clockwise.

ArcSide

Which way a text on an arc reads.

  • "top": The letters stand on the outside of the circle and read clockwise.

  • "bottom": The letters stand upright at the bottom, inside the circle, and read counter-clockwise.

AutoThreshold

The word “auto”.

One of: "auto".

Axis

An axis of the drawing.

One of: "x", "y".

BackOf

Which setup a back setup turns over, and how (docs/specs/two-sided.md §3).

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
registerRegisteryesHow the turned board is put back in place.
setupintegeryesThe id of the front setup: the board as it lies first.
turnTurnyesHow the board is turned over.

Bone

What a chosen corner gets.

One of: "none", "dogbone", "tbone".

ClearingPass

A clearing pass with its own end mill, inside an operation’s shapes and depths: a pocket’s rest pass (docs/specs/rest-passes.md §2: an endmill smaller than the tool before it, clearing only what that tool leaves) or a V-carve’s floor pass (docs/specs/vcarve-wide-strokes.md §4: an end mill clearing the floor between the walls, before the V-carve).

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
enabledbooleannoFalse: kept, but not cut. Missing: true.
entryEntryyesHow the tool goes down: plunge, ramp or helix.
feednumberyesThe cutting feed, mm/min.
plunge_feednumberyesThe feed going down into the material, mm/min.
slot_feednumber or nullnoAs a pocket’s slot_feed. Null: feed.
slot_step_downnumber or nullnoAs a pocket’s slot_step_down. Null: step_down.
step_downnumberyesThe deepest each pass goes, mm.
stepovernumberyesThe distance between the clearing rings, as a fraction of the tool diameter.
toolintegeryesThe id of the project tool it cuts with (tools): an end mill.

CornerChoice

A corner with its own bone, chosen by the user.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
alongWall or nullnoThe wall a T-bone runs along, longer or shorter: the notch is in the other. Required with a T-bone; null otherwise.
at2 × numberyesThe corner, in drawing coordinates (mm).
boneBoneyesnone (the corner stays round), dogbone or tbone.

Corners

How the tool reaches into the inside corners it would leave round, so a square part fits: every corner on the tool’s side up to max_angle.

"type": "dogbone"

In along the corner’s bisector.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
max_anglenumberyesOnly corners this sharp or sharper, radians (1.745 = 100°).

"type": "tbone"

In along the longer wall: the notch is in the shorter one.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
max_anglenumberyesOnly corners this sharp or sharper, radians (1.745 = 100°).

CustomPost

A user post-processor’s config and template, exactly as read from the posts folder.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
configstringyesThe post’s settings file (TOML), as copied.
templatestringyesThe post’s template, as copied.

Direction

One of: "climb", "conventional".

Drawing

The project’s drawing: shapes in millimetres, or (only for jobs from before v4) a reference to a DXF that still has to be embedded.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
dxfslist of DrawingSourcenoThe DXFs the imported shapes came from, each as at its last import or update, with its own placement.
frameslist of FramenoFrames for signs that are drawn again round what they go round when it changes. Left out of the file when there are none, so projects without them open in older versions.
groupslist of ShapeGroupnoGroups of drawn shapes and texts, selected, moved and cut as one. Left out of the file when there are none, so projects without groups open in older versions.
hiddenlist of stringnoGroups and layers not drawn on the canvas (they are still cut): dxf:1, dxf:1/layer:Cut, image:1, text:1, drawn:circle, drawn:rectangle, drawn:polyline.
imageslist of ImagenoPictures traced into shapes, for V-carving.
modelslist of Modelno3D models carved as reliefs from the top. Left out of the file when there are none, so projects without them open in older versions, which refuse one with them.
next_shape_idintegeryesThe id the next new shape gets. Ids are never reused.
shapeslist of ShapeyesThe shapes, imported and drawn.
textslist of TextnoTexts written into shapes, for V-carving, pocketing or profiling. Left out of the file when there are none, so projects without text open in older versions.

DrawingFormat

A drawing file’s format.

One of: "dxf", "svg".

DrawingSource

Provenance of the imported shapes.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
filestringyesThe DXF’s file name.
flattenedobjectnoHow many curves of each type (splines, ellipses) were turned into arcs.
formatDrawingFormatyesWhat kind of file it is: a DXF or an SVG.
idintegeryesThe DXF’s id, which its shapes and selectors name.
ignoredobjectnoHow many entities of each type were left out (text, dimensions, …).
importedobjectnoHow many entities of each type were imported.
issueslist of SourceIssuenoWhat the import found: the import report.
namestringyesIts name in the Browser and messages; the file’s name without its extension at first.
placementPlacementyesHow the imported drawing was moved, turned and scaled on the stock.
px_per_inchnumber or nullnoAn SVG without a real size: the pixels per inch chosen for it, used again on update. None for a DXF and an SVG with real units.
sha256stringyesThe DXF’s SHA-256, to tell whether it changed.
unitsUnitsyesThe DXF’s units: mm or inch. Inch drawings are converted to mm on import.
units_fromUnitsFromyesWhere the units came from: the DXF itself, or the user’s choice.

EdgeSide

Where a chamfer’s waste is: around the shape (a part’s outline) or within it (a hole’s or a pocket’s rim).

One of: "outside", "inside".

Entry

How the tool goes down into the material at the start of each pass, for pockets and follow paths (a profile always spirals: its ramp_angle).

"type": "plunge"

Straight down: needs a centre-cutting tool. An empty struct variant, as Rings.

"type": "ramp"

Down along the path and back, never steeper than ramp_angle (radians, 0 < angle <= 30°).

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
ramp_anglenumberyesThe steepest the ramp goes, radians: more than 0, at most 30° (0.5236).

"type": "helix"

Pockets only: down round a circle of radius (the tool centre’s), never steeper than ramp_angle.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
radiusnumberyesThe helix’s radius, mm: less than the tool radius.
ramp_anglenumberyesThe steepest the helix goes, radians: more than 0, at most 30° (0.5236).

FillPattern

The pattern a PatternOp engraves (docs/specs/fill-pattern.md §3). Angles in radians from the X axis, counter-clockwise; lengths in mm.

  • object: Parallel straight lines, spacing apart (centre to centre), at angle.

  • object: Two sets of lines: at angle, and at angle + between (more than 0, at most 90°).

  • object: The shape’s outline repeated inwards, spacing apart, as closed rings.

  • object: Parallel sine waves at angle, spacing apart: wavelength long, height from top to bottom.

  • object: Rows spacing apart of strokes length long with gap between, every other row shifted half a stroke and gap (docs/specs/fill-pattern.md §14).

  • object: Columns of strokes length long at 45° to angle, spacing apart along the column, every other column mirrored, meeting in chevrons.

  • object: Rows spacing apart of half circles of radius side by side, every other row shifted by a radius.

  • object: Round the middle of each shape: rings spacing apart of strokes length long pointing outwards, pitch apart round the ring, every other ring turned half a pitch.

Finish

A finishing pass on a profile.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
stocknumberyesLeft on the wall by the roughing passes, mm: more than 0, less than the tool radius.

Frame

A frame for a sign that follows what it goes round (docs/specs/sign-maker.md §4): its outline and border lines are ordinary drawn shapes, drawn again from these values when the texts or shapes it goes round change. Editing its shapes by hand ends that: the record goes and they stay as they are.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
aroundFrameAround or nullnoWhat it goes round. None: drawn to a size, following nothing.
border_cornersFrameCorners or nullnoThe borders’ own corner style, for a rectangular frame; None: they follow the outline’s shape, each gap further in (docs/specs/sign-maker.md §5).
border_widthnumbernoThe width of a border’s groove at the surface, mm: the margin is measured to its inner edge, and a double border’s lines are twice it apart. 0: lines as drawn.
borderslist of integernoIts border lines inside the outline, outside in, by shape id.
center2 × numberyesWhere it was last drawn: the outline’s centre, mm.
cornernumberyesThe corner size, mm: what it is depends on the kind (chipload frame kinds).
doublebooleannoEach border two lines, twice border_width apart, which leaves a ridge as wide as a groove between them.
gapnumberyesFrom the outline to the first border, and from one border to the next (from its inner line, for double borders), mm.
heightnumberyesThe outline’s height as last drawn, mm.
kindFrameShapeyesThe outline’s shape.
marginnumberyesFrom the box of what it goes round to its innermost line, mm.
outlineintegeryesIts outline, by shape id; the frame is named by it.
widthnumberyesThe outline’s width as last drawn, mm.

FrameAround

What a frame goes round: texts with all their letters, pictures with all their traced shapes, and other shapes, by id.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
imageslist of integerno
shapeslist of integerno
textslist of integerno

FrameCorners

The corner style of a rectangular frame’s borders (docs/specs/sign-maker.md §5).

One of: "sharp", "round", "notch", "chamfer", "step".

FrameShape

A frame’s outline (docs/specs/shape-library.md §3.1).

One of: "rectangle", "rounded", "notched", "chamfered", "oval", "arched", "plaque", "stepped", "gable", "double_arch", "shield", "banner", "octagon".

HoleMethod

How a Holes operation cuts.

"type": "drill"

Straight down at the hole’s centre, peck mm at a time.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
pecknumber or nullnoDrill this many mm at a time, lifting out between pecks to clear chips. Null: in one go.

"type": "bore"

A helix at the wall, at most step_down mm per turn and never steeper than ramp_angle (radians, 0 < angle <= 30°), then one level circle.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
directionDirectionyesclimb or conventional, as for a profile inside the hole.
ramp_anglenumberyesThe steepest the helix goes, radians: more than 0, at most 30° (0.5236).
step_downnumberyesThe most the helix goes down per turn, mm.

Image

A picture traced into shapes: the picture itself, where it goes, and how it becomes black and white. Its traced shapes are path shapes on its layer that name it in image.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
arcsbooleanyesTraced edges are fitted with arcs as well as lines.
blurnumberyesBlur before the threshold, in pixels, 0 to 10; 0: none.
datastringyesThe picture file itself (PNG, JPEG or BMP), base64-encoded.
filestringyesThe picture’s file name.
idintegeryesThe image’s id, which its traced shapes name.
invertbooleanyesCarve the light parts instead of the dark ones.
layerstringyesThe layer its traced shapes go on.
min_areanumberyesBlack or white specks smaller than this are dropped, mm², 0 to 100.
rotationnumberyesTurned counter-clockwise about the picture’s centre, degrees, at least 0 and less than 360. x, y and width are before the turn; the traced shapes are turned after the trace.
sha256stringyesThe picture’s SHA-256.
smoothingnumberyesHow far a traced edge may be simplified away from the picture’s edge, mm, 0.01 to 1.
thresholdImageThresholdyesGrey values below this (0–255) are black: 1 to 254, or “auto” to pick it from the picture (Otsu’s method).
traceImageTraceyesWhat it is traced into: the black parts’ outlines (for a V-carve) or their centre lines (for a follow path).
widthnumberyesThe picture’s width on the stock, mm; its height follows from the picture.
xnumberyesX of the picture’s lower-left corner on the stock, mm.
ynumberyesY of the picture’s lower-left corner on the stock, mm.

ImageThreshold

Where grey turns black: “auto” or a grey value.

  • AutoThreshold: Picked from the picture.

  • integer: Grey values below this are black, 1 to 254.

ImageTrace

What a picture is traced into.

  • "outline": Closed outlines round the black parts, black inside.

  • "centre_line": Open (or closed) paths along the black strokes’ middles.

InflateProfile

How an inflated shape rises from its edge.

  • "round": A quarter circle: steep at the edge, flat on top.

  • "straight": A straight rise: a ridge along narrow strokes.

Instance

A part placed on a board (docs/specs/parts-and-setups.md §2, instances.md §2): mirrored, then turned, about the part’s centre, then moved.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
anglenumberyesThen turned about the part’s centre, radians, counter-clockwise.
at2 × numberyesThen moved, mm.
enabledbooleanyesFalse: kept, but not cut.
idintegeryesIts number, from 1, unique among the part’s instances on every board; kept when it moves to another board.
mirrorMirror or nullnoMirrored about the part’s centre line first. Null: as drawn.
partintegeryesThe part placed (parts).

InstanceOrder

In which order a part and its instances are cut.

  • "by_operation": Each operation on every instance, then the next operation.

  • "by_instance": Every operation on one instance, then the next instance. Called by_copy before schema 38, and by_part before parts came (schema 22).

IssueSeverity

One of: "warning", "error".

Joint

The joint along a seam between two pieces (docs/specs/pieces.md §4).

  • "knobs": Round heads on narrower necks, alternating in and out, as on a jigsaw puzzle.

  • "fingers": Square teeth with rounded corners.

Lead

Quarter-circle lead-in and lead-out.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
radiusnumberyesRadius of the lead arcs, mm: more than 0, at most 10 tool diameters. Smaller where there’s no room.

LineSegment

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
end2 × numberyesEnd point, mm.
start2 × numberyesStart point, mm.

Machine

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
custom_postCustomPost or nullnoYour own post-processor, copied into the project when chosen, so it gives the same G-code anywhere. Null: post is a built-in one.
gcode_unitsUnitsyesThe units the G-code is written in: mm (G21) or inch (G20). Everything else in the project stays in mm. Since schema 28.
library_uidstring or nullnoThe uid of the machine profile these values came from, for reference; nothing that cuts reads it. Null: not from a profile.
max_feednumber or nullnoThe machine’s fastest feed, mm/min: higher feeds are refused, and the cycle time uses it for rapids. Null: unknown.
namestring or nullnoThe machine profile these values came from, for reference; nothing reads it.
poststringyesThe post-processor: grbl, linuxcnc, mach3, or the name of your own.
safe_znumberyesThe height for rapid moves sideways, mm above Z0. Above the stock top.
spindleSpindleyesThe spindle’s direction: cw (M3) or ccw (M4).
tool_changeToolChangeyesWith several tools: files (one file per tool) or program (one file with tool changes).
travelTravel or nullnoThe machine’s travel, mm. When set, a program that moves farther is refused. Null: not checked.

MaterialRef

A library material, as a project names it.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
library_uidstringyesThe library material’s uid.
namestringyesIts name when chosen, shown when the library no longer has it.

Mirror

How a part is cut mirrored: about the vertical or the horizontal centre line of its box.

  • "left_right": About the vertical centre line: left and right change places, X is mirrored.

  • "top_bottom": About the horizontal centre line: top and bottom change places, Y is mirrored.

Model

A 3D model (an STL file) carved as a relief from the top: the file itself, which way is up, and where it lies on the board. Its footprint outline is a path shape on its layer that names it in model.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
datastringyesThe STL file itself, base64-encoded.
depthnumberyesHow deep the relief goes, from the model’s top to its lowest point, mm.
filestringyesThe STL file’s name.
idintegeryesThe model’s id, which its outline and reliefs name.
keepnumbernoThe share of the turned model’s height kept, from its top, more than 0 and at most 1: what lies below is cut away, its back (docs/specs/relief-wizard.md §5). Width, depth and the outline are the kept part’s. 1 (the whole model) when missing.
layerstringyesThe layer its footprint outline goes on.
namestringyesIts name in the Browser and messages: the file’s name without its extension at first.
picturePictureDepth or nullnoMissing: the model is an STL file. Present: data is a picture (PNG, JPEG or BMP) read as a depth map with these settings; up is z and proportional false.
proportionalbooleanyesThe depth follows the width, in the model’s own proportion: made again whenever the model changes (decision 4).
rotationnumberyesTurned counter-clockwise about the footprint’s centre, degrees, at least 0 and less than 360.
sha256stringyesThe STL file’s SHA-256.
unitsUnitsyesThe unit the file was read in, for its size on import only: the model is placed by width (decision 2).
upModelUpyesWhich of the model’s axes points up on the board.
widthnumberyesThe footprint’s width on the board, mm: it scales X and Y alike.
xnumberyesX of the footprint’s lower-left corner on the board, mm, before the turn.
ynumberyesY of the footprint’s lower-left corner on the board, mm, before the turn.

ModelUp

Which of a model’s axes points up on the board.

One of: "z", "y", "x", "-z", "-y", "-x".

MouldEnds

How a moulding ends on an open path (docs/specs/moulding.md §4).

  • object: The full profile right to the path’s end.

  • object: The profile turned a quarter round the path’s end point.

  • object: The section’s depth fading to the surface over length mm at each end.

MouldProfile

A moulding’s cross-section (docs/specs/moulding.md §3): a built-in shape by its size, or a drawn open shape read as the section.

  • object: A concave quarter circle: radius deep at the path, at the surface radius in.

  • object: A convex quarter circle: the edge rounded, radius down at the path.

  • object: An S, width wide and depth deep: a cove into a round-over, half the width each.

  • object: A raised half round 2 × radius wide, its top at the surface.

  • object: A half-round groove 2 × radius wide and radius deep.

  • object: A drawn open shape, by id: across to the right, up the page; its leftmost point at the path, its highest point the surface.

MouldSide

Which side of the path a moulding’s profile lies on (docs/specs/moulding.md §4).

  • "left": Left of an open path as drawn.

  • "right": Right of an open path as drawn.

  • "inside": Inside a closed path.

  • "outside": Outside a closed path.

Nest

A setup’s nesting settings (docs/specs/nesting.md §10).

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
gapnumberyesThe least distance between what two instances cut, mm.
goalNestGoalnoWhat the nest makes as small as it can (docs/specs/nesting-search.md §2, §6); missing in a file: compact.
marginnumberyesThe least distance from what an instance cuts to the board’s edge, mm.

NestGoal

What a nest makes as small as it can, after placing as much as it can (docs/specs/nesting-search.md §2).

  • "compact": The smallest box round the parts: a block in the board’s lower-left corner.

  • "low": The lowest top edge: a strip across the top of the board left free.

  • "left": The leftmost right edge: a strip down the right of the board left free.

Operation

"type": "profile"

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
clearancenumberyesHow far the cut moves into the material that is kept, mm: positive removes more (a larger pocket, a smaller part), negative leaves material. Less than the tool radius.
corner_choiceslist of CornerChoicenoCorners with their own bone, whatever corners says. Missing or empty: none. Left out when empty.
cornersCorners or nullnoDogbones or T-bones in the inside corners, so square parts fit. Null: the corners stay round.
directionDirectionyesclimb or conventional milling.
enabledbooleannoFalse: the operation stays in the project but isn’t cut. Missing: true.

feednumberyesThe cutting feed, mm/min.
finishFinish or nullnoA finishing pass: the roughing passes leave stock mm on the wall, and one last lap at full depth takes it off. Null: none.
materialMaterialRef or nullnoThe library material its feeds come from. Null: its own values.
namestringnoA name for the operation, shown in the list and on the setup sheet. Empty: none.
partintegeryesThe id of the part it belongs to (parts).
plunge_feednumberyesThe feed going down into the material, mm/min.
ramp_anglenumberyesThe steepest the spiral ramp goes down along the contour, radians: more than 0, at most 30° (0.5236). A profile always spirals down (docs/specs/profile-always-spiral.md).
selectSelectoryesWhat the operation cuts: the shapes on a layer, or shapes by id.
sideSideyesWhich side of the line the tool cuts: outside (cutting a part out), inside (a hole) or on the line.
skinSkin or nullnoAn onion skin: the passes stop thickness mm above the end depth, and one last lap cuts through it. Null: none.
start_depthnumberyesWhere the cut starts, mm down from the stock top: 0 for the top, more when an earlier operation already cut down to there.
startslist of 2 × numbernoWhere the cut starts, as points in drawing coordinates (mm): each moves the start of the ring (contour) nearest to it. Empty: chosen by Chipload.
step_downnumberyesThe deepest each pass goes, mm. The passes are equal, and the last one ends exactly at the end depth.
tabsTabs or nullnoHolding tabs left at the bottom of the cut, so a part cut out doesn’t come loose. Null: none.
throughnumber or nullnoCut through the stock: how far below the stock bottom it ends, mm. The end depth is then the stock thickness plus this, and follows the thickness. Null: the end depth as set.
toolintegeryesThe id of the project tool it cuts with (tools).

jointJointyesThe joints along the seams.
shift_xlist of numbernoEach inner seam between columns moved along X from its even position, mm, from the left. Missing ones: 0.
shift_ylist of numbernoEach inner seam between rows moved along Y from its even position, mm, from the bottom. Missing ones: 0.
sizenumberyesThe joint’s size, mm: a knob’s head diameter, a finger’s length.
spacingnumberyesHow far apart joints are along a seam, mm: knob to knob, or a finger and a gap.

Placement

Where the imported drawing is put: a DXF point p goes to scale · rotate(p) + (dx, dy), turned and scaled about the DXF’s origin.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
dxnumberyesThen moved along X, mm.
dynumberyesThen moved along Y, mm.
rotationnumberyesTurned counter-clockwise about the DXF’s origin, degrees, at least 0 and less than 360, after the scale. Multiples of 90 are exact.
scalenumberyesScaled about the DXF’s origin by this factor, 0.001 to 1000; 1: as drawn.

Plug

A V-carve inlay’s plug (docs/specs/vcarve-inlay.md §2.2).

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
start_depthnumberyesWhere the outline is taken, mm down from the stock top: more than the operation’s start depth, less than its end depth. The end depth less this is the glue gap.

PocketFinish

A finishing pass on a pocket.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
floornumberyesLeft above the end depth by the roughing, mm: 0 or more, at most the step down, less than the cut; 0 on a pocket cut through.
wallnumberyesLeft on every wall by the roughing, mm: 0 or more, less than the tool radius.

RampShape

How a flute’s ramp goes down from its end to the full depth.

  • "straight": Evenly: depth × u, u from 0 at the end to 1 at full depth.

  • "curved": Steepest at the surface, easing into the floor: depth × sin(π/2 × u).

Register

How a turned board is located again.

  • "pins": Dowel pins through holes on the turn axis, drilled with the front.

  • "corner": Pushed into the same corner of a fence: the board’s size must be exact.

ReliefRim

A relief’s rim (docs/specs/stl-relief.md §16): a flat at the floor round the area, then a ramp up to the stock top.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
corner_radiusnumbernobox only: the radius of the flat’s outer corners, mm, from 0 to the width.
hullnumbernooutline only: how far the outline is filled out towards its convex hull before the flat goes round it, 0 (as it is) to 1 (the hull). Missing: 0.
rampnumberyesThe ramp’s slope from the flat up to the stock top, radians from horizontal: from 10° to 90° (a straight wall).
shapeRimShapeyesoutline: the area’s outer loops grown; box: the rectangle round them.
widthnumberyesThe flat’s width, mm from the outline or the rectangle: 0 or more.

ReliefRoughing

A relief’s roughing pass (docs/specs/stl-relief.md §5): the material taken down in levels with the raster, an allowance left on the model for the finish.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
allowancenumberyesWhat is left on the model for the finish, mm, in every direction: 0 or more.
enabledbooleannoFalse: kept, but not cut. Missing: true.
feednumberyesThe cutting feed, mm/min.
plunge_feednumberyesThe feed going down to each stretch’s first point, mm/min.
step_downnumberyesThe distance between levels, mm.
stepovernumberyesThe distance between lines, as a fraction of the tool diameter: more than 0, at most 0.9.
toolintegeryesThe id of the project tool it cuts with (tools): an end mill or a plain ball nose.

ReliefSmoothing

A relief’s smoothing pass (docs/specs/stl-relief.md §17): after the roughing, the surface followed once with the raster, an allowance left on the model for the finish.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
allowancenumberyesWhat is left on the model for the finish, mm, in every direction: 0 or more.
anglenumberyesThe lines’ direction, radians counter-clockwise from X: across the roughing’s by default.
enabledbooleannoFalse: kept, but not cut. Missing: true.
feednumberyesThe cutting feed, mm/min.
plunge_feednumberyesThe feed going down to each line’s first point, mm/min.
stepovernumberyesThe distance between lines, as a fraction of the tool diameter: more than 0, at most 0.9.
toolintegeryesThe id of the project tool it cuts with (tools): an end mill or a plain ball nose.

Rest

Rest machining: the pocket clears only the material the larger tool after_tool can’t reach in its shapes.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
after_toolintegeryesThe id of the larger project tool whose leftovers this pocket clears.

RimShape

What a relief’s rim follows (docs/specs/stl-relief.md §16).

  • "outline": The area’s outer loops, grown with round corners.

  • "box": The rectangle round the area’s outer loops, grown, its corners rounded.

Selector

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
dxfinteger or nullnoOnly shapes from this DXF (an id in drawing.dxfs).
excludelist of integer or nullnoShapes of that DXF, image, text or group left out (ids); with dxf, image, text or group only. A contour with any of them isn’t cut. A traced image’s, or a text’s, are found again when it is made again.
groupinteger or nullnoThe shapes and texts’ letters of this group (an id in drawing.groups), as they are at each run.
imageinteger or nullnoOnly shapes traced from this image (an id in drawing.images).
layerstring or nullnoEvery shape on this layer (upper or lower case don’t matter).
modelinteger or nullnoThe footprint outline of this 3D model (an id in drawing.models).
shapeslist of integer or nullnoThese shapes, by id.
textinteger or nullnoOnly the letters of this text (an id in drawing.texts).

Setup

A setup: one board clamped once on the machine, with its stock and work zero, and the parts cut from it (docs/specs/setups.md §2).

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
back_ofBackOf or nullnoSet: this setup is the back of another, the same board turned over. Its stock and work zero then follow that setup’s ([Job::follow_fronts]), and its cuts are mirrored about the board’s centre line. Null: a board of its own. Since schema 36.
enabledbooleannoFalse: the setup and its parts stay in the project but aren’t cut.
idintegeryesIts number, unique in the project: what parts name it by.
instanceslist of InstanceyesThe parts cut from this board, each placed: a part once where it is drawn, or moved, turned, mirrored, several times. Cut grouped by part in the project’s order, each part’s instances in this order. Since schema 41, in place of each part’s own placement and instances.
namestringyesIts name, shown in the Browser, the report and the setup sheet.
nestNest or nullnoHow the setup was last nested: its gap and margin, kept so Nest again needs no questions. Missing: never nested. Left out when missing.
piecesPieces or nullnoSet: the setup is cut as a puzzle, in pieces that each fit a board, joined by seams with joints; each piece is a program of its own. Null: one board. Since schema 37.
stockStockyesThe board.
work_zeroWorkZeronoThe point of the drawing that becomes X0 Y0 in its G-code. Missing: the drawing’s origin.

Shape

One shape of the drawing: imported from a DXF (path: lines and arcs, one shape per DXF entity), or drawn in Chipload (circle, rectangle, polyline). One flat object: id, layer, kind, then the fields of that kind.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
anglenumber or nullnorectangle: its rotation about the centre; polygon: the angle of its first corner from +X; radians, counter-clockwise.
bulgeslist of number or nullnopolyline: one per segment, as in DXF: tan(sweep / 4); 0 straight, 1 a counter-clockwise half circle, -1 clockwise.
center2 × number or nullnocircle, rectangle, polygon: the centre, mm.
closedboolean or nullnopath, polyline, spline: whether it ends where it starts.
constructionbooleannocircle, rectangle, polygon, polyline: construction geometry, shown and snapped to but never cut; left out when false.
corner_radiusnumber or nullnorectangle: the radius of its corners, mm; 0 for sharp ones.
dxfinteger or nullnopath: the DXF it was imported from (its id in drawing.dxfs).
dxf_handlestring or nullnopath: the handle of the DXF entity it came from, which keeps its id when the DXF is updated.
dxf_typestring or nullnopath: the DXF entity’s type, e.g. LWPOLYLINE.
idintegeryesThe shape’s id, which operations select it by.
imageinteger or nullnopath: the image it was traced from; absent for shapes from a DXF.
kindShapeKindyespath (imported from a DXF), or a shape drawn in Chipload: circle, rectangle, polyline, polygon, spline.
layerstringyesIts layer.
lengthnumber or nullnorectangle: its size along its own X axis, mm.
modelinteger or nullnopath: the 3D model it is the footprint outline of (its id in drawing.models).
namestring or nullnocircle, rectangle, polyline: its name in the Browser; left out when it has none.
pointslist of 2 × number or nullnopolyline: its points; spline: the points the curve passes through; mm.
radiilist of number or nullnopolyline: a radius at each point, mm, rounding the corner there with an arc tangent to both sides (0 sharp); left out when every corner is sharp.
radiusnumber or nullnocircle: the radius, mm; polygon: the radius to its corners, mm.
segmentslist of PathSegment or nullnopath: its lines and arcs, mm.
sidesinteger or nullnopolygon: how many sides (and corners) it has, 3 to 1000.
textinteger or nullnopath: the text it is a letter of (its id in drawing.texts).
widthnumber or nullnorectangle: its size along its own Y axis, mm.

ShapeGroup

Drawn shapes and texts kept together: one click selects them all, and they move, turn, scale and are cut as one (docs/specs/shape-groups.md). A shape or text is in at most one group; groups don’t hold groups.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
idintegeryesThe group’s id, which operations’ selectors name.
namestringyesIts name in the Browser.
rotationnumbernoHow far its box is turned, degrees counter-clockwise, in [0, 360): the turns it was given, so that its box turns with it (docs/specs/dxf-whole-on-canvas.md §6). 0 for a new group.
shapeslist of integernoIts drawn shapes, by id.
textslist of integernoIts texts, by id, with their letters.

ShapeKind

Side

One of: "inside", "outside", "on".

Skin

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning

SourceIssue

Stock

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
fitnumber or nullnoCentred on the drawing only: its size is the drawing’s outline plus this margin each side, mm, following the drawing; x and y are then used only while the drawing is empty. Null: the size is x × y.
materialMaterialRef or nullnoThe project’s material: new operations take it, with its values. Each operation names its own (material on the operation). Null: none.

StockCorner

One of: "center", "bottom_left", "bottom_right", "top_left", "top_right".

StockOrigin

  • "bottom_left", "center":

  • "drawing": Centred on the drawing’s outline, sticking out equally all round, and following the drawing when it moves or changes (the user, 2026-10-28). Construction geometry doesn’t count; an empty drawing centres it on the origin.

Tabs

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
alongAxis or nullnoWhere a ring without tabs placed by hand gets its count tabs: on its edges along x (bottom and top) or y (left and right), half on each. Left out: evenly round it.
atlist of 2 × numbernoTab centres placed by hand, in drawing coordinates (mm): a contour with points gets exactly those tabs, one without gets count, evenly spaced. Left out when empty.
countintegeryesTabs per contour, at least 1.
flank_anglenumberyesThe slope of the tab’s sides, radians: π/2 (90°) straight, less for sloped tabs a ramped profile follows.
heightnumberyesThe bridge’s thickness, up from the bottom of the cut, mm.
widthnumberyesThe bridge left on the part, along its edge, mm (the flat top of a sloped tab).

Text

A text written into shapes: what it says, its font and size, and where it goes. Its letters are path shapes on its layer that name it in text, made again when it changes; the font is needed only for that, not to cut them.

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
alignTextAlignyesHow the lines line up against each other.
anchorTextAnchornoWhat x and y are: the box’s lower-left corner (before the turn), or its centre. A centred text stays centred there when its words, font or size change. Missing: the corner.
arcTextArc or nullnoWritten along a circle round its position (docs/specs/text-on-arc.md §4); None: on straight lines. Optional, no schema bump: an older Chipload refuses the field.
fontstringyesThe font: a built-in one (FreeSans, FreeSerif, …) or one on the computer, by its full name (chipload text fonts). Only its name is stored; its letters are the text’s shapes, kept where the font isn’t (docs/specs/system-fonts.md).
heightnumberyesThe height of a capital letter, mm.
idintegeryesThe text’s id, which its letters name.
layerstringyesThe layer its letters go on.
line_spacingnumberyesFrom one line’s base to the next, in capital heights.
rotationnumberyesTurned counter-clockwise about the box’s centre, degrees, at least 0 and less than 360.
spacingnumberyesExtra space between letters, mm; 0: the font’s own spacing, kerning included.
textstringyesWhat it says; a line break (“\n”) starts a new line.
xnumberyesX of the text’s anchor on the stock, mm: the lower-left corner of its box (before the turn) or its centre.
ynumberyesY of the text’s anchor on the stock, mm.

TextAlign

How a text’s lines line up.

One of: "left", "center", "right".

TextAnchor

What a text’s position is (docs/specs/text.md §3).

  • "corner": The lower-left corner of the text’s box, before the turn.

  • "center": The centre of the text’s box (the turn keeps it in place).

TextArc

A text on an arc: the circle through its letters’ middles and where on it the text sits (docs/specs/text-on-arc.md §2).

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
anglenumberyesWhere the text sits, degrees counter-clockwise from +X: its middle, start or end as it is aligned.
radiusnumberyesThe radius of the circle through the first line’s letters’ middles (half a capital height above its base), mm; more than 0.
sideArcSideyesAlong the top (standing outside, read clockwise) or the bottom (upright, read counter-clockwise).

Tool

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
anglenumber or nullnoV-bits: the included angle, radians. Null: unknown; a V-carve then refuses the tool. Ball noses: the taper’s included angle; null: a plain ball nose.
center_cuttingboolean or nullnoWhether it can plunge straight down. False: profiles ramp in and pockets go down round a helix. Null: unknown.
corner_radiusnumber or nullnoEnd mills: the radius of its corners, mm, a bull nose (docs/specs/corner-radius.md §2). Missing: square corners. Left out of the file when missing, so a Chipload that doesn’t know it refuses only a tool that has one.
diameternumberyesDiameter, mm. For a V-bit, its widest cutting diameter.
flutesinteger or nullnoNumber of flutes, for the chip load. Null: unknown.
idintegeryesThe tool’s number: what operations name it by, and its T number in the G-code.
library_uidstring or nullnoThe uid of the library tool it came from, for reference; nothing that cuts reads it. Null: not from the library.
namestring or nullnoThe library tool it came from, for reference.
rpmnumber or nullnoSpindle speed, rpm. Needed to generate.
tip_diameternumber or nullnoV-bits: the flat tip’s diameter, mm. Null: pointed. Tapered ball noses: the ball’s diameter at the tip.
typeToolTypenoendmill, drill, vbit or ballnose.

ToolChange

How a job with several tools is written.

  • "files": One G-code file per tool group; the user changes the tool and sets Z zero between.

  • "program": One file with T<n> M6 at each change, for a sender that handles it.

ToolType

  • "endmill", "drill", "vbit":

  • "ballnose": A ball nose, plain or tapered (docs/specs/stl-relief.md §8): for reliefs and follow paths only.

Travel

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
xnumberyesTravel along X, mm.
ynumberyesTravel along Y, mm.
znumberyesTravel along Z, mm.

Turn

How a board is turned over: about its vertical or its horizontal centre line.

  • "left_right": About the vertical centre line: the left edge goes to the right, X is mirrored.

  • "top_bottom": About the horizontal centre line: the bottom edge goes to the top, Y is mirrored.

Turns

How a part may be turned when it is nested (docs/specs/nesting.md §3): wood grain is the usual reason for none or half.

  • "none": As it lies.

  • "half": As it lies or turned 180°.

  • "quarter": A quarter turn at a time.

  • "every_15": 15° at a time.

Units

One of: "mm", "inch".

UnitsFrom

  • "dxf": Declared by the file: a DXF’s $INSUNITS, an SVG’s size in real units.

  • "user": The file declared none; the user chose (a DXF’s units, an SVG’s pixels per inch).

Wall

One of a corner’s two walls, by length.

One of: "longer", "shorter".

WorkZero

Where the G-code’s X0 Y0 is. The geometry never moves; only the output is translated.

  • object: The drawing’s own origin (the only choice before work_zero existed). An empty struct variant, not a unit one, so serde refuses stray fields here too.

  • object: A corner or the centre of the stock, as placed by stock.origin.

  • object: Any point, in drawing coordinates (mm).

ZZero

One of: "top", "bottom".

Tool library file

written_bystring or nullnoThe Chipload version that wrote the file.

Post-processors

A post-processor is a small TOML file (name.toml) and a Jinja template (name.*.j2); see Post-processors for writing your own. The TOML file’s fields:

The file

FieldTypeRequiredMeaning
arcsbooleannoWrite arcs as G2/G3; false writes them as short lines (G1). Missing: false.
controllerslist of stringnoThe controllers the post is for, as post list names them, e.g. GRBL, grblHAL. Missing: none named (docs/specs/more-posts.md §2).
decimalsintegernoDecimals for coordinates and feeds in mm. Missing: 3.
dwell_unitDwellUnitnoThe unit of the dwell’s P: s (seconds) or ms (milliseconds, MASSO). Missing: seconds (docs/specs/more-posts.md §4).
file_extensionstringnoThe G-code file’s extension, e.g. .nc. Missing: .nc.
inch_decimalsintegernoDecimals for coordinates and feeds in inches. Missing: 4.
namestringyesThe post’s display name, e.g. “GRBL”.
notestring or nullnoA word for the operator, shown on the setup sheet, e.g. that the post isn’t tested on a real controller yet. Missing: none.
schema_versionintegeryesThe version of the settings format: 1.
spindle_dwell_snumberyesSeconds to wait after starting the spindle, for it to reach speed; 0 for none. Required: a silent default could start cutting at low speed.
templatestringyesThe template’s file name, next to this file, e.g. myrouter.nc.j2.
tool_changeslist of ToolChangenoHow the template can write a job with several tools: files (one program per tool), and program (one file with tool changes) if the template writes them. Missing: files only.
unitslist of UnitsnoThe units the template can write: mm (G21) and inch (G20) if it writes {{ units_word }} instead of a fixed G21. Missing: mm only, so a machine set to inch is refused rather than given inch numbers under G21.
written_bystring or nullnoWho or what wrote the file, for reference.

DwellUnit

The unit a controller reads a dwell’s P in.

  • "s": Seconds (LinuxCNC, GRBL, Centroid CNC12).

  • "ms": Milliseconds (MASSO).

ToolChange

How a job with several tools is written.

  • "files": One G-code file per tool group; the user changes the tool and sets Z zero between.

  • "program": One file with T<n> M6 at each change, for a sender that handles it.

Units

One of: "mm", "inch".

linuxcnc.ngc3G2/G33filesNot yet tested on a real LinuxCNC machine: check the program and air-cut first.
mach3.tap3G2/G33filesNot yet tested on a real Mach3 or Mach4 machine: check the program and air-cut first.
masso.nc3G2/G33filesNot yet tested on a real MASSO machine: check the program and air-cut first.
acorn.cnc3G2/G33filesNot yet tested on a real Centroid machine: check the program and air-cut first.

Troubleshooting

Importing

Message or symptomWhat to do
Asked for mm or inchThe DXF doesn’t record its units. Choose the ones it was drawn in. In CAD, set the drawing units ($INSUNITS) before exporting.
invalid_contour: a contour crosses or folds back on itselfFix it in CAD: a closed contour must not cross itself. Often a line drawn twice with a tiny offset, or a polyline that doubles back.
open_contour on a shape that should be closedIts ends don’t meet within 0.001 mm. Join them in CAD (snap the end points), or use Follow path if it really is a line.
branch_pointThree or more loose lines on one layer meet at one point, so Chipload can’t tell which way a contour goes. Remove the extra line in CAD, or draw the shape as one closed polyline.
Text is missingTEXT entities aren’t read. Convert text to outlines (curves) in CAD.
A DWG fileNot supported. Save it as DXF.

Generating

MessageWhat to do
tool (diameter … mm) does not fit in the pocketThe pocket is narrower than the tool everywhere. Use a smaller tool.
the tool does not fit through a narrow partA warning: the pocket is cleared as separate areas, and material stays in the narrow part. A rest pass with a smaller tool can clear it.
uncut materialThe pocket’s stepover is too large for its shape. Lower the stepover.
end depth … is … below the stock …, more than the allowedThe cut is deeper than Max depth below stock allows. Check the stock thickness, or raise the allowance (Machine).
Tool N has no spindle speedSet the tool’s spindle speed (rpm).
a v-bit with no angleSet the V-bit’s angle: click the tool in the Browser.
shapes … no longer exist; choose its geometry againAfter updating the DXF, an operation’s shapes are gone. Select new ones, then Use selection.

The check failed

The checker refused the program, so nothing was exported. The message names the line and the rule:

  • Rapid into material: usually the stock’s thickness or Z zero is set wrong for the depths, or the safe Z is below the stock top.
  • Machine travel: the program is larger than the machine’s travel. Check the work zero and the drawing’s position.
  • With your own post: a code it writes that the checker doesn’t accept. See Post-processors.

The part comes out the wrong size

  • Units: a drawing in inches read as mm (or the other way) is 25.4 times off. Check the units in the import report.
  • Oversize: a positive oversize (clearance in the file) makes parts smaller and holes larger. See Depths and oversize.
  • Side: an outside profile on a hole, or the other way, is off by a tool diameter. Check each profile’s side.
  • Tool diameter: Chipload offsets by the diameter you set. Measure the real tool.

Languages

The app speaks English, German (Deutsch), French (Français), Spanish (Español), Italian (Italiano), Swedish (Svenska) and Russian (Русский).

Choosing the language

At the start the app follows your computer’s language, and English when it isn’t one of these. To choose another, use Edit > Preferences…, which opens Preferences in the Browser. The change is immediate, and the app keeps it for next time.

Problems, the report, the checker’s findings, error messages and the setup sheet follow the language too. What stays in English, whatever the language:

  • The G-code and its comments.
  • The command line, --json output and the AI assistant tools (a setup sheet written by chipload sheet --lang or chipload run --sheet-lang excepted).
  • The issues found when a DXF was first read, which the project keeps as they were written, and what other programs say (the file system, a picture that can’t be read).
  • This guide.

Names you give things (parts, operations, tools, machines, layers) are yours and are never translated. Numbers keep the decimal point in every language, and a field also takes a decimal comma (6,5, 0,3) in every language. In English only, a comma followed by exactly three digits after a whole number (1,000, 2,500) isn’t read, since it could mean a thousand: type 2.5 or 2,5 there. In a language that writes a decimal comma, every comma in a sum is one: 12,5+3,5.

Helping with a translation

German, French, Spanish, Italian and Russian are marked (review wanted) in the list of languages: they haven’t yet been read by someone who speaks the language and runs a CNC machine. A wrong word in a CAM program can mislead (climb or conventional, inside or outside), so your corrections matter.

The texts are plain files in the source, one folder per language: crates/chipload-i18n/locales/<language>/*.ftl. The machining terms used all through them are in crates/chipload-i18n/GLOSSARY.md. Open an issue with the wrong text and a better one, or send a pull request that changes the file.
